因此，当这些长成的、生命力完全的神秘主义者试图向我们讲述他们已达成的生命时，他们所描述的总是一种极度活跃的生命。他们并不说自己「安居于安息的享受之中」——虽然对这一点的深邃而喜乐的认识，或许还有对在其中全然自失的永久渴望，是始终为他们所拥有的——而是说，他们「沿着默观之梯上行，*也下行*」。他们向上朝着那至高之点伸展——那独一的实在，生命的一切错综而多彩的线都流向它、并在其中融合；又冲出去，朝着那些多样的生命，怀着主动之爱与服务的激情。这双重活动，这安息与工作之间的摆荡——唯有这个，他们说，才真正是人的生命；因为唯有这个，在人的层面上代表了一点他们在上帝之中发现的那无穷丰富却又单纯的生命——「永远行动，却永远安息」。人一旦达至此境，就确实实现了他与实在的合一；因为此时他已成为那永恒创造行动的一部分，那神圣思想与爱之永恒生成的一部分。因此，默观即使在它最高、最亲、最内在的程度上，也不该是你的终点本身。唯有当它催促你去行动时，它才真正属于你：唯有当超越之爱的双重运动——向内收敛，达于合一与享受；又向外奔涌，达于创造的行动——在你之内实现时。你要成为那至高艺术家藉以工作的、一件活的炽热工具：是他自我彰显的诸般器官之一，是那把他的实在带进具体表达的永恒进程的一环。

===Original===
Now the expression of vision, of reality, of beauty, at an artist’s hands—the creation of new life in all forms—has two factors: the living moulding creative spirit, and the material in which it works. Between these two there is inevitably a difference of tension. The material is at best inert, and merely patient of the informing idea; at worst, directly recalcitrant to it. Hence, according to the balance of these two factors, the amount of resistance offered by stuff to tool, a greater or less energy must be expended, greater or less perfection of result will be achieved. You, accepting the wide deep universe of the mystic, and the responsibilities that go with it, have by this act taken sides once for all with creative spirit: with the higher tension, the unrelaxed effort, the passion for a better, intenser, and more significant life. The adoration to which you are vowed is not an affair of red hassocks and authorised hymn books; but a burning and consuming fire. You will find, then, that the world, going its own gait, busily occupied with its own system of correspondences—yielding to every gust of passion, intent on the satisfaction of greed, the struggle for comfort or for power—will oppose your new eagerness; perhaps with violence, but more probably with the exasperating calmness of a heavy animal which refuses to get up. If your new life is worth anything, it will flame to sharper power when it strikes against this dogged inertness of things: for you need resistances on which to act. “The road to a Yea lies through a Nay,” and righteous warfare is the only way to a living and a lasting peace.

Further, you will observe more and more clearly, that the stuff of your external world, the method and machinery of the common life, is not merely passively but actively inconsistent with your sharp interior vision of truth. The heavy animal is diseased as well as indolent. All man’s perverse ways of seeing his universe, all the perverse and hideous acts which have sprung from them—these have set up reactions, have produced deep disorders in the world of things. Man is free, and holds the keys of Hell as well as the keys of Heaven. Within the love-driven universe which you have learned to see as a whole, you will therefore find egotism, rebellion, meanness, brutality, squalor: the work of separated selves whose energies are set athwart the stream. But every aspect of life, however falsely imagined, can still be “saved,” turned to the purposes of Reality: for “all-thing hath the being by the love of God.” Its oppositions are no part of its realness; and therefore they can be overcome. Is there not here, then, abundance of practical work for you to do; work which is the direct outcome of your mystical experience? Are there not here, as the French proverb has it, plenty of cats for you to comb? And isn’t it just here, in the new foothold it gives you, the new clear vision and certitude—in its noble, serious, and invulnerable faith—that mysticism is “useful”; even for the most scientific of social reformers, the most belligerent of politicians, the least sentimental of philanthropists?

To “bring Eternity into Time,” the “invisible into concrete expression”; to “be to the Eternal Goodness what his own hand is to a man”—these are the plainly expressed desires of all the great mystics. One and all, they demand earnest and deliberate action, the insertion of the purified and ardent will into the world of things. The mystics are artists; and the stuff in which they work is most often human life. They want to heal the disharmony between the actual and the real: and since, in the white-hot radiance of that faith, hope, and charity which burns in them, they discern such a reconciliation to be possible, they are able to work for it with a singleness of purpose and an invincible optimism denied to other men. This was the instinct which drove St. Francis of Assisi to the practical experience of that poverty which he recognised as the highest wisdom; St. Catherine of Siena from contemplation to politics; Joan of Arc to the salvation of France; St. Teresa to the formation of an ideal religious family; Fox to the proclaiming of a world-religion in which all men should be guided by the Inner Light; Florence Nightingale to battle with officials, vermin, dirt, and disease in the soldiers’ hospitals; Octavia Hill to make in London slums something a little nearer “the shadows of the angels’ houses” than that which the practical landlord usually provides.

===Chinese===
在艺术家手中，对异象、实在与美的表达——以一切形式创造新生命——含有两个要素：活的、塑造着的创造之灵，以及它在其内工作的质料。这两者之间，必然有一种张力的差异。质料顶好也不过是惰性的，仅仅容受那赋形于它的观念；最坏时，则直接抗拒它。因此，依照这两个要素之间的消长、依照质料向工具提供的阻力的多寡，所须耗费的能量便有大有小，所得成果的完全程度也有高有低。你接受了神秘主义者那广阔深邃的宇宙，连同随之而来的种种责任，便因这一举动一劳永逸地站到了创造之灵一边：站在更高的张力、毫不松懈的努力、对一种更好、更强烈、更有意义的生活的热忱一边。你已许身的那敬拜，不是红跪垫与官定赞美诗集的事务；而是一团燃烧吞噬的火。于是你将发现，这世界照它自己的步调行走，忙于它自己那套对应系统——向每一阵情欲的狂风屈身，一心满足贪婪，争夺安适或权势——将要反对你新的热望；或许以暴烈的方式，但更可能以一种令人恼火的沉静，像一头拒绝起身的沉重的牲畜。你那新的生命若果有点价值，当它撞上事物这种顽强的惰性时，便要燃起更锐利的力量：因为你需要可以施力的阻力。「通往『是』的道路，必穿过『否』」；而正义的争战，乃是通往活的、持久的和平的唯一道路。

再者，你将越来越清楚地观察到，你外部世界的质料、公共生活的方法与机器，不只被动地、更是主动地与你那内在的锐利真理视象不相容。那沉重的牲畜不只怠惰，还患着病。人观看其宇宙的一切乖僻方式，以及从其中生出的一切乖僻丑恶的行为——这些已激起种种反作用，在事物的世界中造成深的紊乱。人是自由的，手里握着天堂的钥匙，也握着地狱的钥匙。于是，在你已学会视为一个整体的、那被爱所驱动的宇宙之内，你将发现利己、悖逆、卑鄙、残暴、污秽：那些分离的自我——其能量横挡在川流之上——的作为。但生命的每一面向，无论被想象得多么错误，都仍可以「被救赎」，被转向实在的诸般目的：因为「*万有*皆因上帝之爱而有其存在」。它的种种对立并不是它的真实性的一部分；因此它们能够被克服。那么，这里岂不是有大量实际的工作留给你去做；这工作正是你神秘经验的直接产物？这里岂不正如法国谚语所说，由你梳理的猫还多得很？而神秘主义之所以「有用」，岂不正在这里：在它给你的新立足点、新的清晰视象与确信之中——在它那高贵、严肃、无懈可击的信仰之中；即便对最科学化的社会改革者、最好斗的政客、最不感伤的慈善家，也是如此。

「把永恒带进时间」，把「不可见者带进具体的表达」；「对永恒之善作他的手，正如人自己的手之于人」——这些是一切伟大神秘主义者明白表述的愿望。他们无一例外，都要求恳切而深思的行动，要求把那净化了的、炽热的意志插进事物的世界。神秘主义者是艺术家；而他们最常工作的质料，就是人的生活。他们要医治那实然与真实之间的不和谐；并且，由于在那燃烧于他们之内的信、望、爱德的白热光辉之中，他们辨明这样的和解是可能的，他们便能以一种单一的目的与一种不可征服的乐观为它工作——这是他人所没有的。正是这本能，驱使阿西西的圣方济各去实际体验那被他认作至高智慧的贫穷；驱使锡耶纳的圣加大利纳从默观走向政治；驱使圣女贞德去拯救法兰西；驱使圣德兰去建立一个理想的宗教大家庭；驱使福克斯去宣告一种人人受内光引导的世界宗教；驱使弗洛伦斯·南丁格尔去与伤兵医院里的官吏、害虫、污秽与疾病作战；驱使奥克塔维娅·希尔在伦敦的贫民窟里，造出比讲求实际的房东通常提供的略微更近于「天使之屋的荫影」的东西。

===Original===
All these have felt sure that a great part in the drama of creation has been given to the free spirit of man: that bit by bit, through and by him, the scattered worlds of love and thought and action shall be realised again as one. It is for those who have found the thread on which those worlds are strung, to bring this knowledge out of the hiddenness; to use it, as the old alchemists declared that they could use their tincture, to transmute all baser metals into gold.

So here is your vocation set out: a vocation so various in its opportunities, that you can hardly fail to find something to do. It is your business to actualise within the world of time and space—perhaps by great endeavours in the field of heroic action, perhaps only by small ones in field and market, tram and tube, office and drawing-room, in the perpetual give-and-take of the common life—that more real life, that holy creative energy, which this world manifests as a whole but indifferently. You shall work for mercy, order, beauty, significance: shall mend where you find things broken, make where you find the need. “*Adoro te devote, latens Deitas*” said St. Thomas in his great mystical hymn: and the practical side of that adoration consists in the bringing of the Real Presence from its hiddenness, and exhibiting it before the eyes of other men. Hitherto you have not been very active in this matter: yet it is the purpose for which you exist, and your contemplative consciousness, if you educate it, will soon make this fact clear to you. The teeming life of nature has yielded up to your loving attention many sacramental images of Reality: seen in the light of charity, it is far more sacred and significant than you supposed. What about *your* life? Is that a theophany too? “Each oak doth cry I *am*,” says Vaughan. Do you proclaim by your existence the grandeur, the beauty, the intensity, the living wonder of that Eternal Reality within which, at this moment, you stand? Do your hours of contemplation and of action harmonise?

If they did harmonise—if everybody’s did—then, by these individual adjustments the complete group-consciousness of humanity would be changed, brought back into conformity with the Transcendent; and the spiritual world would be actualised within the temporal order at last. Then, that world of false imagination, senseless conflicts, and sham values, into which our children are now born, would be annihilated. The whole race, not merely a few of its noblest, most clearsighted spirits, would be “in union with God”; and men, transfused by His light and heat, direct and willing agents of His Pure Activity, would achieve that completeness of life which the mystics dare to call “deification.” This is the substance of that redemption of the world, which all religions proclaim or demand: the consummation which is crudely imagined in the Apocalyptic dreams of the prophets and seers. It is the true incarnation of the Divine Wisdom: and you must learn to see with Paul the pains and disorders of creation—your own pains, efforts, and difficulties too—as incidents in the travail of that royal birth. Patriots have sometimes been asked to “think imperially.” Mystics are asked to think celestially; and this, not when considering the things usually called spiritual, but when dealing with the concrete accidents, the evil and sadness, the cruelty, failure, and degeneration of life.

So, what is being offered to you is not merely a choice amongst new states of consciousness, new emotional experiences—though these are indeed involved in it—but, above all else, a larger and intenser life, a career, a total consecration to the interests of the Real. This life shall not be abstract and dreamy, made up, as some imagine, of negations. It shall be violently practical and affirmative; giving scope for a limitless activity of will, heart, and mind working within the rhythms of the Divine Idea. It shall cost much, making perpetual demands on your loyalty, trust, and self-sacrifice: proving now the need and the worth of that training in renunciation which was forced on you at the beginning of your interior life. It shall be both deep and wide, embracing in its span all those aspects of Reality which the gradual extension of your contemplative powers has disclosed to you: making “the inner and outer worlds to be indivisibly One.” And because the emphasis is now forever shifted from the accidents to the substance of life, it will matter little where and how this career is actualised—whether in convent or factory, study or battlefield, multitude or solitude, sickness or strength. These fluctuations of circumstance will no longer dominate you; since “it is Love that payeth for all.”

===Chinese===
这一切人都确信：在创造的戏剧中，一个伟大的角色已经派给了人自由的灵；那散落的爱、思想与行动诸世界，将一点一点地、借着并经由他，重新实现为合一的一体。凡已找到那串起这些世界的线的人，就当把这知识从隐藏中带出来；运用它——如古代炼金术士宣称他们能运用他们那点金之药那样——把一切较贱的金属化为黄金。

这样，你的圣召已在此陈明：这圣召的机会如此多样，你几乎不可能找不到可做的事。你的本分，是在时间与空间的世界之内实现那更真实的生命、那神圣的创造能量——这个世界对它的彰显，虽属整体，却属漠然——或许借英雄行动领域中的伟大努力，或许只借田野与市集、电车与地铁、办公室与客厅中的微小努力，借公共生活中那不停的施与受。你要为怜悯、秩序、美、意义而工作：在哪里发现事物破损，就在那里修补；在哪里发现需要，就在那里创造。圣托马斯在他那首伟大的神秘颂歌中说：「*Adoro te devote, latens Deitas*」（我虔敬朝拜你，隐匿的神性）；而这敬拜的实际一面，就在于把真在从隐藏中带出来，陈展在他人眼前。在这件事上你迄今并不怎么积极；然而这正是你存在的目的，你的默观意识——你若加以教育——不久便会把这一事实向你讲明。自然界那丰饶的生命，已向你那满有爱的注意交出实在的许多圣事性形象：在爱德的光中所见，它远比你想象的更神圣、更有意义。那么*你的*生命呢？它岂不也是一次神性显现（theophany）？「每棵橡树都呼喊：我*在*」，沃恩说。你是否借你的存在，宣告那永恒实在——你此刻正站立于其中的——的宏伟、美、强度、活的奇迹？你的默观之时与行动之时，是否和谐？

倘若它们果真和谐——倘若人人如此——那么，借着这些个别的调适，人类那完整的群体意识就会改变，被带回与超越者相合的状态；灵性世界终于要在时间秩序之内得到实现。那时，那虚假想象、无谓冲突与虚伪价值的世界——我们的儿女如今就生在其中——将被灭除。全人类，而不只是它那些最高贵、目光最明澈的少数精灵，都将「与上帝合一」；人们既被他的光与热所浸透，作他纯粹活动的直接而甘愿的行动者，就必达成那生命的完全，即神秘主义者敢于称之为「神化」的完全。这就是那世界救赎的实体，一切宗教都宣告它或要求它：这就是那终极的成全，先知与先见们只曾在启示式的梦中对它作过粗陋的想象。这是神圣智慧真正的道成肉身；你必须学会同保罗一起，把受造界的痛苦与紊乱——连同你自己的痛苦、努力与艰难——看作那王者降生之阵痛中的诸般插曲。爱国者有时被要求「以帝国为念来思考」；神秘主义者则被要求「以属天之事为念来思考」；而这思考，不在思量那些通常被称为属灵之事的时候，倒在他们处理具体的偶发事件、生命的邪恶与忧伤、残酷、失败与堕落的时候。

这样，摆在你面前的，不只是让你在新的意识状态、新的情感经验之间作一选择——虽然这些确实包含在内——而首先是，一种更广大、更炽烈的生命，一项事业，一种对实在之利益的全然奉献。这生命不该是抽象而梦似的，不该如某些人所想象的那样由种种否定构成。它该是强烈地实际而肯定的；给意志、心灵与心智的无限活动留出用武之地，让它们在神圣理念的诸节律之内运作。它将索价甚高，对你的忠贞、信赖与自我牺牲提出不停的要求：如今便要证明，那在你内在生活之初强加于你的弃绝训练，是何等必要、何等有价值。它既深且广，在其跨度之内涵摄你默观能力的逐步扩展已向你显明的一切实在面向：「使内在与外在的世界不可分地合为一」。而且，既然重点如今已永远从生活的偶性移到生活的实体，这项事业在哪里、以何种方式实现，都无关紧要——无论在修院或工厂、书房或战场、人群或独处、患病或健壮之时。这些境遇的波动将不再支配你；因为「是为一切偿付的，乃是爱」。

===Original===
Yet by all this it is not meant that the opening up of the universe, the vivid consciousness of a living Reality and your relation with it, which came to you in contemplation, will necessarily be a constant or a governable feature of your experience. Even under the most favourable circumstances, you shall and must move easily and frequently between that spiritual fruition and active work in the world of men. Often enough it will slip from you utterly; often your most diligent effort will fail to recapture it, and only its fragrance will remain. The more intense those contacts have been, the more terrible will be your hunger and desolation when they are thus withdrawn: for increase of susceptibility means more pain as well as more pleasure, as every artist knows. But you will find in all that happens to you, all that opposes and grieves you—even in those inevitable hours of darkness when the doors of true perception seem to close, and the cruel tangles of the world are all that you can discern—an inward sense of security which will never cease. All the waves that buffet you about, shaking sometimes the strongest faith and hope, are yet parts and aspects of one Ocean. Did they wreck you utterly, that Ocean would receive you; and there you would find, overwhelming and transfusing you, the unfathomable Substance of all life and joy. Whether you realise it in its personal or impersonal manifestation, the universe is now friendly to you; and as he is a suspicious and unworthy lover who asks every day for renewed demonstrations of love, so you do not demand from it perpetual reassurances. It is enough, that once it showed you its heart. A link of love now binds you to it forevermore: in spite of derelictions, in spite of darkness and suffering, your will is harmonised with the Will that informs the Whole.

We said, at the beginning of this discussion, that mysticism was the art of union with Reality: that it was, above all else, a Science of Love. Hence, the condition to which it looks forward and towards which the soul of the contemplative has been stretching out, is a condition of *being*, not of *seeing*. As the bodily senses have been produced under pressure of man’s physical environment, and their true aim is not the enhancement of his pleasure or his knowledge, but a perfecting of his adjustment to those aspects of the natural world which concern him—so the use and meaning of the spiritual senses are strictly practical too. These, when developed by a suitable training, reveal to man a certain measure of Reality: not in order that he may gaze upon it, but in order that he may react to it, learn to live in, with, and for it; growing and stretching into more perfect harmony with the Eternal Order, until at last, like the blessed ones of Dante’s vision, the clearness of his flame responds to the unspeakable radiance of the Enkindling Light.

===Chinese===
然而，这一切并不意味着：那在默观中临到你的宇宙之开启——对一个活的实在及其与你之关系的鲜活意识——必会成为你经验中一个恒常的、可以驾驭的特征。即使在最有利的情况之下，你也必得、也必须在那种灵性享受与人间世界中的积极工作之间，从容而频繁地往来。它常常会从你手中全然溜走；你那最勤勉的努力也常不能重新把它捕获，只有它的芬芳留存。那些接触曾经越是强烈，当它们这样被撤走时，你的饥饿与荒凉就越发可怕：因为感受性的增长，意味着更多的痛苦，也意味着更多的快乐——每一位艺术家都知道这一点。但在临到你的一切事上，在一切敌对你、使你忧伤的事上——甚至在那些不可避免的黑暗时辰，当真实知觉之门似乎已经关闭，你所能辨明的只有世界那残酷的纠结——你将发现一种内在的安稳感，永不止息。那一切冲击你、颠簸你、有时连最强的信与望也为之动摇的波浪，仍是一个大洋的部分与面向。纵使它们把你全然撞碎，那大洋也必收纳你；在那里你将发现那一切生命与喜乐的不可测度的本体，覆没你、浸透你。无论你在其位格性的还是非位格性的彰显中领悟它，宇宙如今对你都是友善的；正如一个天天要求爱情重新证明的爱人，是个多疑而不配的爱人，你也照样不向它要求无休止的再保证。它曾一度向你显示它的心，这就够了。如今有一条爱的链索把你与它永远系在一起：纵有遭弃之感，纵有黑暗与苦难，你的意志已与那赋予整体以生命的意志和谐了。

在这番讨论的开端，我们说过：神秘主义是与实在合一的艺术；说它首先是爱的科学。因此，它所盼望的那境况——默观者的灵魂一直向之伸展的那境况——是一种*存在*的境况，而非*观看*的境况。正如身体感官是在人物理环境的压力下产生的，它们的真正目的不在于增进他的快乐或知识，而在于使他与自然世界中那与他有关的诸面向之间的调适得以完全——灵性感官的运用与意义，也同样严格地是实际的。这些感官经适当的训练而发展之后，便向人启示出某种量度的实在：不是为了让他凝视它，而是为了让他对它作出回应，学会活在它之内、与它同在、并为它而活；生长并伸展，与永恒秩序进入更完全的和谐，直到最后——像但丁异象中的那些有福者一样——他火焰的澄明，回应着那点燃之光不可言说的光辉。
